I first-person imperfect: I seek refuge, take shelter, cling for protection, or enter a protected relation\",\"prose\":\"{{ar:أَعُوذُ}} ({{tr:aʿūdhu}}) is the first-person verb inside the commanded quote, so the addressed hearer becomes the one saying, \\\"I seek refuge.\\\" Its imperfect form makes refuge current and renewable rather than a completed past act. The root image is not bare fear: the ʿ-w-dh field brings seeking shelter, clinging, and entry into protection, while the following {{ar:بِرَبِّ ٱلنَّاسِ}} ({{tr:bi-rabbi n-nāsi}}) gives that motion a named protector. Protective-object and refuge-place derivatives also keep the uttered formula from sounding like description only; under the command, saying it participates in entering protection, while those derivatives remain background pressure. The verb also withholds the object of danger in this ayah; the refuge act is established before the whispering threat is disclosed later in the surah, especially in 114:4-6. Form evidence keeps agency straight: the local Form I says the speaker seeks refuge, not that the speaker grants refuge as a causative protector. Cross-reference pressure is concrete rather than generic: human-linked refuge is rerouted from seeking refuge with jinn in 72:6 to seeking refuge with the Lord of humans here, and the adjacent opening in 113:1 shows the same refuge formula with a changed title. Sound reinforces the syntax, as the long central vowel stretches the refuge verb after the clipped command and liaison can soften the move from command into response.\",\"root_display\":\"{{ar:ع و ذ}} ({{tr:ʿ-w-dh}})\",\"root_gloss_range\":\"broad ʿ-w-dh range includes seeking refuge, clinging for safety, protective shelter, fortification, and protective formulae; the local imperfect selects the speaker's renewed refuge-taking act\",\"surface_display\":\"{{ar:أَعُوذُ}} ({{tr:aʿūdhu}})\"}","trust":"trusted"},{"branch_refs":[],"citable":true,"role":"candidate_evidence","scope":"micro","source_local_id":"114:1:2:human-refuge-reversal","source_type":"word_analysis","support_id":"sup_2e53629ac76bfcf1cc8e","text":"{\"blocking_evidence\":null,\"headline\":\"human-linked refuge reverses the jinn refuge route\",\"reader_payoff\":\"The reader notices a pointed contrast with 72:6: refuge connected to humans is redirected from created beings to the Lord named over humans.\",\"reason\":\"The concrete reference to 72:6 supports contrast, while the local grammar keeps {{ar:بِرَبِّ ٱلنَّاسِ}} ({{tr:bi-rabbi n-nāsi}}) as the refuge source here.\",\"representative_source_ids\":[\"QI-9c479e0e\",\"QH-cbe29d0b\"],\"status\":\"used\"}","trust":"trusted"},{"branch_refs":[],"citable":true,"role":"candidate_evidence","scope":"micro","source_local_id":"114:1:3","source_type":"word_analysis","support_id":"sup_2ebe691b5f328efcd668","text":"{\"gloss_range\":\"bi-governed construct title: by, with, or in attachment to the Lord of humankind as the refuge source\",\"prose\":\"{{ar:بِرَبِّ}} ({{tr:bi-rabbi}}) is the hinge of the clause. The prefixed {{ar:بِـ}} ({{tr:bi-}}) ties the word back to {{ar:أَعُوذُ}} ({{tr:aʿūdhu}}) as the one by whom and with whom refuge is sought, while {{ar:رَبِّ}} ({{tr:rabbi}}) simultaneously governs {{ar:ٱلنَّاسِ}} ({{tr:an-nāsi}}) as its construct complement. That double-facing grammar makes the refuge source both dependent in the verb phrase and governing in the title phrase. The articleless construct is not an indefinite free title; definiteness comes through the following definite human noun, so the phrase means the Lord in relation to humankind. The r-b-b root image brings authority joined to developmental care: ownership, mastery, sustaining, rearing, and bringing a domain toward completion. The condensed-syrup derivative is only root-image pressure, not the selected sense, but it helps preserve the idea of enrichment and maturation inside the title. Distribution and echo add local weight: {{ar:رَبِّ}} ({{tr:rabbi}}) is a stable noun-title, pairs tightly with refuge formulas at 113:1, 23:97, 23:98, 11:47, and 3:36, and begins the repeated title chain that continues with 114:2 and 114:3. Its position also sets up the same-surah contrast: 114:1 names the refuge source, while 114:5 locates the whispering threat inside human breasts. Even the sound participates, with the doubled b of {{ar:رَبِّ}} ({{tr:rabbi}}) forming a compact center before the assimilated boundary into {{ar:ٱلنَّاسِ}} ({{tr:an-nāsi}}).\",\"root_display\":\"{{ar:ر ب ب}} ({{tr:r-b-b}})\",\"root_gloss_range\":\"broad r-b-b range includes owning, mastering, governing, sustaining, rearing, developing, and bringing to completion; the local noun selects the divine Lord-title in construct with humankind\",\"surface_display\":\"{{ar:بِرَبِّ}} ({{tr:bi-rabbi}})\"}","trust":"trusted"},{"branch_refs":[],"citable":true,"role":"candidate_evidence","scope":"micro","source_local_id":"114:1:3:form-fusion-and-title-stability","source_type":"word_analysis","support_id":"sup_3bf220919ed07b6298bf","text":"{\"blocking_evidence\":null,\"headline\":\"fused form and distribution stabilize the title\",\"reader_payoff\":\"The reader notices that the written form visibly fuses the help-marker to a highly stable noun-title rather than presenting a loose independent word.\",\"reason\":\"The word is analyzed as a preposition plus noun, and contextual evidence gives {{ar:ر ب ب}} ({{tr:r-b-b}}) overwhelmingly as a divine noun-title in this form class.\",\"representative_source_ids\":[\"QF-8fafec51\",\"QI-3592e0d1\"],\"status\":\"used\"}","trust":"trusted"},{"branch_refs":[],"citable":true,"role":"candidate_evidence","scope":"micro","source_local_id":"114:1:4","source_type":"word_analysis","support_id":"sup_40107a1c49436fcb8e85","text":"{\"gloss_range\":\"definite collective noun in genitive: humankind, people as a generic human totality\",\"prose\":\"{{ar:ٱلنَّاسِ}} ({{tr:an-nāsi}}) closes the ayah by naming the domain of the refuge title. Its article makes the noun definite and generic, and its collective singular shape lets the verse speak of humankind as one shared class rather than a list of individuals. Because it is the genitive complement of {{ar:رَبِّ}} ({{tr:rabbi}}), it also sends definiteness back into the construct phrase: the title becomes the Lord of the human domain. The root-image material is useful but must be narrowed. The ʾ-n-s field can press sociability, familiarity, perception, and companionship into the word, while disputed alternatives add restlessness or forgetfulness as vulnerability images; none of those replaces the local sense, but together they make the protected domain feel like social, perceiving, exposed human beings rather than a bare population count. The reported ibdāl variant tests consonant shape without changing the humankind referent. The same noun then becomes the surah's lexical spine, recurring in 114:2, 114:3, 114:5, and 114:6: first under lordship, then kingship and deity, and later as the place where whispering reaches. The Lord-human pairing is species-specific, narrower than the broader Lord-of-worlds formula in 1:2, and cross-reference pressure from 72:6 sharpens the route of refuge because humans once seeking refuge with jinn are here placed under the Lord of humans. Sound also helps the word hold the ending: article assimilation binds it to the construct head, while the repeated n-s closure becomes the acoustic endpoint of the surah's title chain.\",\"root_display\":\"{{ar:أ ن س}} ({{tr:ʾ-n-s}})\",\"root_gloss_range\":\"broad ʾ-n-s range includes human sociability, familiarity, perception, and intimacy;
